<<<SUMMARY>>>
How are investors evaluating the next phase of artificial intelligence growth, and what role will data centers, semiconductor companies, and hyperscalers play in shaping the future of technology markets? In this NYSE Live interview, R "Ray" Wang, CEO and Principal Analyst of Constellation Research, discusses the impact of election outcomes on technology markets, AI infrastructure investment, semiconductor competition, hyperscaler spending, and the outlook for major technology companies. Kristen Scholer spoke with R "Ray" Wang about artificial intelligence, data center expansion, semiconductor demand, hyperscaler investment, Apple, AMD, and the broader technology sector. A major theme throughout the conversation is the future of AI infrastructure. Wang notes that data center development remains essential to supporting artificial intelligence growth, but regulatory and community concerns could influence the pace of expansion in certain markets. The interview also explores the competitive dynamics between the United States and China in AI infrastructure development. Data center capacity, energy availability, and regulatory approvals continue to play important roles in determining how quickly AI ecosystems can scale. Another key topic is the evolving role of public policy. Wang believes discussions surrounding regulation, environmental standards, and economic benefits will shape how communities approach future data center projects. The conversation further examines the semiconductor landscape, with particular attention on AMD. Wang highlights the company's progress in both CPUs and AI-focused infrastructure solutions, arguing that its competitive position has strengthened significantly in recent years. A significant focus of the discussion is AMD's growth trajectory. According to Wang, the company's EPYC and Instinct product families have helped expand its presence in enterprise computing and AI-related markets. The interview also explores the broader implications of hyperscaler spending. Major technology companies continue investing aggressively in infrastructure, cloud computing, and artificial intelligence despite concerns about rising capital expenditures and near-term profitability. Another major theme is investor perception of negative free cash flow. Wang argues that some hyperscale technology companies are making deliberate investments today in pursuit of larger long-term opportunities across AI and cloud platforms. The discussion also examines major cloud and infrastructure providers, including companies investing heavily in AI capacity, advanced computing, and future growth initiatives. Additionally, Wang discusses Apple's position within the AI race. Rather than spending as aggressively on large-scale infrastructure, Apple has focused on integrating AI capabilities into devices and consumer experiences across its ecosystem. The interview further examines Apple's latest earnings performance, including iPhone demand, Mac sales, services revenue, and the company's broader product cycle. Another key topic is device innovation and upgrade activity. Wang believes Apple continues to benefit from a significant replacement cycle that could support future demand across its product portfolio. The conversation also highlights the concept of a technology market rotation. While AI infrastructure remains important, investors are increasingly evaluating where long-term value may emerge across semiconductors, cloud providers, software platforms, and connected technologies. A significant theme is the attractiveness of hyperscalers. Wang argues that despite concerns around spending and valuation, some of the largest technology companies may still offer compelling long-term opportunities due to the scale of their AI investments. The discussion further explores broader economic considerations including inflation, energy production, oil prices, and the role macroeconomic conditions play in shaping technology investment trends. Looking ahead, Wang expects AI infrastructure, cloud computing, semiconductors, and enterprise technology innovation to remain major drivers of market growth while investors continue evaluating the long-term return potential of large-scale AI investments. Throughout the discussion, R "Ray" Wang shares insights on artificial intelligence, data centers, semiconductor markets, cloud computing, technology investing, digital infrastructure, enterprise technology, Apple, AMD, and the future of AI-driven growth. headline: Taiwan Semi or Broadcom: If I Could Only Own 1 for the Next 5 Years, It Would Be This
<<<SUMMARY>>>
The article compares Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing (TSMC) and Broadcom as AI infrastructure plays. While both stocks have tripled in three years, the author favors TSMC because it manufactures chips for multiple competing designers (Nvidia, AMD, Apple, Broadcom) and doesn't depend on any single winner. TSMC's decades of manufacturing expertise and ability to produce advanced chips at scale provide a more defensible competitive advantage than Broadcom's custom chip design business, which faces competition and customer concentration risks.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
TSM | positive | Favored as the superior long-term investment due to its diversified customer base across competing chip designers, decades of manufacturing expertise, high barriers to entry in advanced chip production, and ability to benefit regardless of which AI chip designer wins market share.
AVGO | neutral | Acknowledged as an outstanding company with considerable growth potential in AI infrastructure and custom chip design, but faces higher execution risk due to customer concentration, competition from Nvidia and others, and dependence on continued customer investment in proprietary processors.
NVDA | neutral | Mentioned as a competitor to Broadcom in AI accelerators and as a major customer of TSMC, representing one of multiple potential winners in the AI chip market.

headline: Why Advanced Micro Devices Stock Popped Today
<<<SUMMARY>>>
AMD stock surged 8.43% following CoreWeave's strong Q3 earnings beat and raised guidance for 2026, signaling increased capital spending on semiconductor chips. Additionally, a UBS report highlighted that server CPU shipments jumped 31% year-over-year in Q3, with AMD maintaining steady market share at 28.4% while Intel declined. UBS projects CPU sales could reach $210 billion by 2030, potentially generating $60 billion in revenue for AMD alone.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
AMD | positive | Stock up 8.43% due to CoreWeave's strong earnings and capital spending plans that will increase AMD chip purchases, combined with UBS report showing AMD maintaining market share while competitors decline and CPU prices rising, with bullish long-term projections to 2030.
CRWV | positive | Company beat Q3 earnings expectations with lower losses and higher revenue than forecast, raised full-year 2026 guidance, and announced higher-than-expected capital investment spending of $37-39 billion.
INTC | negative | UBS report indicates Intel experienced a 240-basis-point decline in CPU market share in Q3, losing ground to competitors like AMD in the server CPU market.
